\begin{lemma}[One block, two laws]
\label{lem:two-laws}
Assume the conclusion of \cite[Section~6]{Allikvere_2026}. There is $c'>0$ such that for all sufficiently large $x$ and $y\in\{x^\alpha,x^{\alpha^2}\}$
\begin{align}
 \mathbb P\bigl(\tau_x(\mathbf U_y)=\infty\bigr)&\ll x^{-c'},\label{eq:two-laws-hit}\\
 \sup_{E}\bigl|\mathbb P\bigl(\operatorname{Pass}_x(\mathbf U_y)\in E\bigr)-\mathbb P\bigl(\operatorname{Pass}_x(\mathbf L_y)\in E\bigr)\bigr|&\ll(\log x)^{-c'},\label{eq:two-laws-tv}
\end{align}
where $E$ runs over the sets of odd integers in $[1,x]$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By the transfer, \eqref{eq:two-laws-hit} is \cite[(D.1)]{Allikvere_2026} for $T_{3,c}$. For a set $E$ of odd integers in $[1,x]$ and $m\le m_0$ put
\[
 S_m(E)=\sum_{M\in E'(E)}\frac{3^m\,p_m(M\bmod 3^m)}{M},
\]
and let $E_x$ be the set of all odd integers in $[1,x]$. By \cite[Section~6.4]{Allikvere_2026} for $T_{3,c}$,
\[
 \mathbb P\bigl(\operatorname{Pass}_x(\mathbf U_y)\in E\bigr)=\bigl(1+O(\log^{-c'}x)\bigr)\frac{S_{m_1}(E)}{S_{m_1}(E_x)}+O(\log^{-c'}x),
\]
with $S_{m_1}(E_x)\asymp1$; the normaliser comes from $E=E_x$, for which the probability is $1$ by the convention $\operatorname{Pass}_x=1$ on no hit. By \cite[(5.7), (5.20), (5.21)]{Tao_2022} for $T_{3,c}$, normalised in the same way, the same holds for $\mathbf L_y$ with $m_0$ in place of $m_1$. It remains to compare $S_{m_0}$ and $S_{m_1}$. For $N\in\mathbb Z/3^{m_0}\mathbb Z$ put $C(N)=\sum_{M\in E',\,M\equiv N\ (3^{m_0})}3^{m_0}/M$. The argument of \cite[Step~3 in the proof of Proposition~3.5]{GGM_2025}, with $n=2m_0$, gives $C(N)\ll1$ uniformly in $N$. Hence, uniformly in $E$,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \bigl|S_{m_0}(E)-S_{m_1}(E)\bigr|
 &\le\sum_{N\in\mathbb Z/3^{m_0}\mathbb Z}\Bigl|p_{m_0}(N)-3^{m_1-m_0}p_{m_1}(N\bmod3^{m_1})\Bigr|\,C(N)\\
 &\ll\operatorname{Osc}_{m_1,m_0}\bigl(p_{m_0}(N)\bigr)_N\ll_Am_1^{-A}
 \end{aligned}
\]
by \cite[Proposition~1.14]{Tao_2022}, which holds for $c\cdot\mathbf{Syrac}$ by the transfer. As $m_1\asymp\log^{0.4}x$, the two normalised profiles differ by $O(\log^{-c'}x)$, and \eqref{eq:two-laws-tv} follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:natural}]
For $3\mid c$ we reduce to $c'=c/3$ as in Step~0 of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:logdens}: an odd $n$ lies in $A$ if and only if $(n+c')/2$ lies in $A'=\{m:3m\in A\}$, and $n\mapsto(n+c')/2$ maps the odd integers below $Y$ onto the integers in $[(1+c')/2,(Y+c')/2)$, so that a natural density of $A'$ gives one of the odd part of $A$. Let now $3\nmid c$. For large $N$ put $x=N^{1/\alpha^2}$ and $y=x^\alpha$, so that $\mathbf U_y$ is uniform on the odd integers in $[N^{1/\alpha},N]$. By~\eqref{eq:passage} for $\mathbf U_y$ and for $\mathbf L_y$, by \eqref{eq:two-laws-hit}, by \cite[(1.19)]{Tao_2022} for $T_{3,c}$ and by \eqref{eq:two-laws-tv} with $E=A_1\cap[1,x]$,
\[
 \bigl|\mathbb P(\mathbf U_y\in A_1)-\mathbb P(\mathbf L_y\in A_1)\bigr|\ll(\log x)^{-c'}.
\]
As in the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:natural}, $\mathbb P(\mathbf L_y\in A_1)\to\delta(A)$, the odd integers below $N^{1/\alpha}$ are a proportion $O(N^{1/\alpha-1})$ of those below $N$, and the sets $\{2^km:m\text{ odd}\}$ carry the same relative density. Hence $A$ has the natural density $\delta(A)$.

For a predecessor set $P(a)$, $3\nmid c$, the same argument applies to $A_1=P(a)\cap(2\N+1)$. The first-passage invariance~\eqref{eq:passage} now fails only on the event $\operatorname{Pass}_x=a'$, with $a'$ the odd part of $a$, as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:potential}(a). Its probability under $\mathbf U_y$ is at most its probability under $\mathbf L_y$ plus $O(\log^{-c'}x)$ by~\eqref{eq:two-laws-tv}. Under $\mathbf L_y$ it is $O(\log^{-c'}x)$, because \cite[(5.12)--(5.15)]{Tao_2022} for $T_{3,c}$ give $\operatorname{Pass}_x(\mathbf L_y)\ge x\exp(-O(\log^{0.6}x))>a'$ outside an event of that probability. The numbers $2^ia$ form a set of natural density $0$. Hence $P(a)$ has the natural density $h(a)$.
\end{proof}


\section{The \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-adic Syracuse law: \texorpdfstring{$L^p$}{Lp} regularity and Fourier decay}
\label{sec:syracuse-lp}
The Syracuse profile $f_n$ of Remark~\ref{rem:spikes}(d) is the reference object of Theorem~\ref{thm:lower}, Conjecture~\ref{conj:profile} and Table~\ref{table:ui}. Its densities converge in $L^1$ \cite[(1.26)]{Tao_2022}, and it has no square-integrable density (Remark~\ref{rem:spikes}(d)). This appendix reduces its $p$-integrability for $1<p<2$ to a single $L^2$ quantity and identifies where its self-correlations become infinite. It then reports the exact laws up to $n=20$ (Run~L) and a Monte Carlo study of its largest Fourier coefficients up to $n=80$ (Run~M). Finally it rewrites the energy in a logarithmic chart, determines the full singular set of the correlations, shows that the maps entering the energy avoid it (Run~N), and reduces the main term of the energy to a mixing statement (Run~O). Multiplication by the unit $c$ permutes the residues and the frequencies. So $H_p(n)$, the energies below and the set of values of $|\chi_n|$ on the units do not depend on $c$, and we take $c=1$.

Let $a_1,a_2,\dots$ be independent with $\mathbb P(a_i=k)=2^{-k}$, $A_j=a_1+\dots+a_j$, and $S_n=\sum_{j=1}^n3^{j-1}2^{-A_j}\bmod3^n$. Reversing the order of the $a_i$ gives Tao's $\mathbf{Syrac}(\mathbb Z/3^n\mathbb Z)$. Moreover $S_n\bmod3^{n-1}=S_{n-1}$ and $S_n=2^{-a}(1+3S'_{n-1})$ with an independent copy $S'_{n-1}$. Let $f_n=3^n\,\mathbb P(S_n=\cdot)$, $H_p(n)=\mathbb E_xf_n(x)^p$ over the uniform $x\in\mathbb Z/3^n\mathbb Z$, and $\chi_n(\xi)=\mathbb E\,e(\xi S_n/3^n)$. The densities form a martingale for the $3$-adic filtration. Hence $H_p(n)$ increases with $n$, and $\sup_nH_p(n)<\infty$ for some $p>1$ means that the limiting density lies in $L^p(\mathbb Z_3)$. By Parseval and $\chi_n(3\xi)=\chi_{n-1}(\xi)$,
\[
 E_n:=H_2(n)-H_2(n-1)=\sum_{\xi\in(\mathbb Z/3^n\mathbb Z)^\times}|\chi_n(\xi)|^2 ,
\]
the $L^2$ energy of the unit frequencies.

\begin{lemma}[Self-similarity]
\label{lem:selfsim}
Put $\psi_a(x)=(2^ax-1)/3\bmod3^{n-1}$ for $x\equiv2^{-a}\pmod3$. Then
\[
 f_n(x)=\sum_{a\ge1}3\cdot2^{-a}\,\mathbf 1[x\equiv2^{-a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 3)]\,f_{n-1}(\psi_a(x)).
\]
Each $\psi_a$ maps the class $\{x\equiv2^{-a}\pmod3\}$ bijectively onto $\mathbb Z/3^{n-1}\mathbb Z$. If $a\equiv b\pmod2$, then $\psi_b=g_{b-a}\circ\psi_a$ on this class, where $g_d(y)=2^dy+(2^d-1)/3$ for even $d$. The maps $g_d$ are affine isometries of $\mathbb Z_3$, and $g_{-d}=g_d^{-1}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$\mathbb P(S_n=x)=\sum_a2^{-a}\,\mathbb P(1+3S'_{n-1}\equiv2^ax\pmod{3^n})$, which requires $2^ax\equiv1\pmod3$ and then equals $\mathbb P(S'_{n-1}=\psi_a(x))$. For $x=2^{-a}(1+3y)$ we have $\psi_b(x)=(2^{b-a}(1+3y)-1)/3=g_{b-a}(y)$.
\end{proof}

For an affine isometry $h(y)=uy+t$ of $\mathbb Z_3$ ($u$ a unit) let
\[
 \operatorname{Cor}_m(h)=\mathbb E_yf_m(y)f_m(h(y))=3^m\,\mathbb P\bigl(S'_m\equiv h(S_m)\pmod{3^m}\bigr),
\]
with independent $S_m$, $S'_m$. Thus $\operatorname{Cor}_m(\mathrm{id})=H_2(m)$ and $\operatorname{Cor}_m(h^{-1})=\operatorname{Cor}_m(h)$.

\begin{proposition}[The second moment]
\label{prop:energy}
For $n\ge2$, $E_n=2\sum_{d\ge2\text{ even}}2^{-d}\operatorname{Cor}_{n-1}(g_d)$. More generally, for every affine isometry $h(y)=uy+t$,
\[
 \operatorname{Cor}_n(h)=3\sum_{a,b\ge1}2^{-a-b}\,\mathbf 1\bigl[2^{b-a}u+2^bt\equiv1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 3)\bigr]\operatorname{Cor}_{n-1}(h_{a,b}),
\]
where $h_{a,b}(y)=2^{b-a}u\,y+(2^{b-a}u+2^bt-1)/3$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write $S=2^{-a}(1+3S')$ and $T=2^{-b}(1+3T')$. The congruence $T\equiv h(S)$ reads $1+3T'\equiv2^{b-a}u(1+3S')+2^bt$. Modulo $3$ this is the indicator condition, and then it says $T'\equiv h_{a,b}(S')\pmod{3^{n-1}}$. For $h=\mathrm{id}$ the condition is $a\equiv b\pmod2$ and $h_{a,b}=g_{b-a}$. The terms with $a=b$ give $3\sum_a4^{-a}H_2(n-1)=H_2(n-1)$. For each even $d\ne0$, the pairs with $b-a=d$ carry the weight $3\sum_{a\ge1}2^{-2a-|d|}=2^{-|d|}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The singular set]
\label{prop:singular}
For every integer $e\ne0$ and the map $y\mapsto2^ey$, $\operatorname{Cor}_n(y\mapsto2^ey)\ge2^{-|e|}H_2(n-1)\to\infty$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $u=2^e$, $t=0$, the pairs with $b-a=-e$ satisfy the condition and give $h_{a,b}=\mathrm{id}$. Their weight is $2^{-|e|}$, and all terms are nonnegative.
\end{proof}

This is the overlap principle of Remark~\ref{rem:spikes}(d) in correlation form. Multiplying by $2^e$ shifts the first valuation, so the law of $2^eS$ contains $2^{-|e|}$ times the law of $S$. The maps $g_d$ differ from $y\mapsto2^dy$ by the translation $(2^d-1)/3$, which is nonzero and has $3$-adic valuation $v_3(d/2)$.

\begin{lemma}[Kahane--Peyri\`ere inequality]
\label{lem:kp}
For $1<p\le2$ and $n\ge1$,
\[
 H_p(n)\le\lambda_p\,H_p(n-1)+E_n^{p/2},\qquad\lambda_p=\frac{3^{p-1}}{2^p-1}.
\]
For $p=2$ this is the identity $H_2(n)=H_2(n-1)+E_n$. Since $\log(2^p-1)$ is strictly concave and $\lambda_1=\lambda_2=1$, we have $\lambda_p<1$ for $1<p<2$ (for example $\lambda_{1.5}=0.947$).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $y_a(x)=3\cdot2^{-a}\mathbf 1[x\equiv2^{-a}]f_{n-1}(\psi_a(x))$, so that $f_n=\sum_ay_a$. As $s\mapsto s^{p/2}$ is subadditive,
\[
 f_n^p=\Bigl(\sum_ay_a^2+\sum_{a\ne b}y_ay_b\Bigr)^{p/2}\le\sum_ay_a^p+\Bigl(\sum_{a\ne b}y_ay_b\Bigr)^{p/2}.
\]
By the bijections of Lemma~\ref{lem:selfsim}, $\mathbb E_xy_a^p=\frac13(3\cdot2^{-a})^pH_p(n-1)$, and these sum to $\lambda_pH_p(n-1)$. By Jensen's inequality, the mean of the last term is at most $(\mathbb E_x\sum_{a\ne b}y_ay_b)^{p/2}$. The case $p=2$ of the same computation shows that $\mathbb E_x\sum_{a\ne b}y_ay_b=E_n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}
\label{cor:lp}
If $E^*=\sup_nE_n<\infty$, then $\sup_nH_p(n)\le1+(E^*)^{p/2}/(1-\lambda_p)$ for every $1<p<2$. So the limiting density lies in $L^p(\mathbb Z_3)$ for every $p<2$ and, by Remark~\ref{rem:spikes}(d), not in $L^2$: the critical exponent is $2$. If only $E_n\le Cn^\theta$, then $H_p(n)=O(n^{\theta p/2})$, and the $L^q$ dimensions of the Syracuse measure equal $1$ for $1<q\le2$.
\end{corollary}

For independent copies in place of $f_{n-1}\circ\psi_a$, the recursion of Lemma~\ref{lem:selfsim} is a multitype smoothing transform with spectral radius $\lambda_p$ at order $p$ \cite{Durrett_Liggett_1983,Kahane_Peyriere_1976}. Since $\lambda_2=1$, its tail $\mathrm{Haar}\{f>M\}\asymp M^{-2}$ sits exactly at the critical exponent. Lemma~\ref{lem:kp} replaces the missing independence by the single number $E_n$.

\begin{conjecture}[Bounded energy]
\label{conj:energy}
$\sup_nE_n<\infty$; equivalently, by Proposition~\ref{prop:energy}, $\sup_m\sum_d2^{-d}\operatorname{Cor}_m(g_d)<\infty$.
\end{conjecture}

The recursion of Proposition~\ref{prop:energy} is a positive linear recursion on the affine maps. Its identity is fixed with weight $\lambda_2=1$, which is the source of the linear growth of $H_2$. After Runs~L and~M, this appendix rewrites $E_n$ in a logarithmic chart (Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral}), determines the full singular set of the recursion (Proposition~\ref{prop:intertwiners}), and states a decorrelation conjecture (Conjecture~\ref{conj:decor}) that implies Conjecture~\ref{conj:energy}.

\paragraph{Run~L} We computed the laws of $S_n$ exactly up to floating point for $n\le20$. The law at level $n$ is a cyclic correlation in the discrete logarithms of the units modulo $3^n$ ($2$ is a primitive root), evaluated by FFT. At $n=20$ this took $1.6$ hours and $205$~GB. The identity of Proposition~\ref{prop:energy} holds to all printed digits, and Lemma~\ref{lem:kp} holds at every level, for $p=1.5$ with slack at least $0.36$ (Table~\ref{table:syr-moments}). The increments of $E_n$ fall from $5.7\cdot10^{-4}$ at $n=15$ to $3.1\cdot10^{-4}$ at $n=20$, by a factor of about $0.87$ per level, so $E_n$ converges to about $0.4751$. The increments of $H_p(n)$ shrink by the factors $0.909$, $0.930$ and $0.959$ per level at $n=20$ for $p=1.25$, $1.5$ and $1.75$, while $H_2$ grows by $E_n$ per level. The correlations $\operatorname{Cor}_m(g_d)$ change by at most $0.014$ from $m=8$ to $m=20$, and by at most $0.003$ from $m=16$ to $m=20$. At $m=20$ they are $0.73$, $0.47$, $0.86$, $0.43$ for $d=2$, $4$, $8$, $14$ ($v_3(d/2)=0$); $1.24$, $1.37$ for $d=6$, $12$; and $2.11$, $2.36$, $2.78$ for $d=18$, $54$, $162$. So they grow by about $0.47$ per unit of $v_3(d/2)$, as the main term $H_2(s)-\frac12E_{s+1}$ of an exact formula for $d=2\cdot3^sm$, $3\nmid m$, predicts (research note \texttt{syracuse\_lp}). For the translation $y\mapsto y+3^j$, $\operatorname{Cor}_{20}-H_2(j)$ lies between $-0.30$ and $-0.15$ for $1\le j\le19$. For $y\mapsto2^ey$, $e=1$, $2$ and $4$, the correlation grows by $0.237$, $0.119$ and $0.030$ per level, that is by $2^{-e}E_n$ as Proposition~\ref{prop:singular} predicts. With the translations $1$, $9$, $81$ added, it stays constant. The tail $\mathrm{Haar}\{f_{20}>M\}$ equals $9.6\cdot10^{-3}$, $1.2\cdot10^{-3}$, $8.5\cdot10^{-5}$ and $5.4\cdot10^{-6}$ for $M=10$, $30$, $100$ and $300$, with local exponents $1.9$, $2.2$ and $2.5$ steepening towards the cut-off $\max f_{20}=4862$.

\midrule

\paragraph{Fourier maxima (Run~M)} By the identity for $E_n$, $|\chi_n|$ is of size $3^{-n/2}$ on average over the units, and Conjecture~\ref{conj:energy} says exactly this. Its maximum $\chi^*_n=\max_{\xi\text{ unit}}|\chi_n(\xi)|$ decays much more slowly. Tao proves $\chi^*_n\ll_An^{-A}$ for every $A$ \cite[Proposition~1.17]{Tao_2022} and writes that the bound could perhaps be improved ``to as far as $O(\exp(-cn))$''. Toguchi computed $\chi^*_n$ up to $n=21$ \cite{Toguchi_2026}. He found the maximiser $\xi=2^k$ with $k\approx4(n-1)/3$, and a power law $n^{-2.1}$ fitted better than an exponential.

We estimated $\chi_n(2^k)$ by Monte Carlo with exact $128$-bit arithmetic for $S_n$, computed by Horner's rule from the inside. The two runs had $10^{11}$ samples each and a standard error of $3.2\cdot10^{-6}$. They agree with the exact values of Run~L for $16\le n\le20$ within $1.4$ standard errors. The first run, with a window of $13$ exponents around $4(n-1)/3$, showed that the maximiser follows $n\log_23$ instead: $k^*-n\log_23\in[-6.6,-5.3]$ for $n\le42$. For $n=41$ and all $n\ge43$ its maximum sat at the window edge. The second run recentred the window at $\operatorname{round}(n\log_23)-6$ ($17$ exponents, $36\le n\le80$). There the maximum never reaches the edge, and $k^*-n\log_23\in[-7.5,-5.2]$. So $\xi\approx3^n/64$, a fixed fraction of the modulus, and for $\xi=2^k$ the recursion
\[
 \chi_n(2^k)=\sum_{a\le k}2^{-a}\,e\bigl(2^{k-a}/3^n\bigr)\chi_{n-1}\bigl(2^{k-a}\bigr)+\sum_{a>k}(\dots)
\]
acts on the relative frequency $2^k/3^n$ by multiplication with $3\cdot2^{-a}$. The first run for $16\le n\le35$ and the second for $36\le n\le80$ together give $65$ levels with $\chi^*_n\ge20$ standard errors, from $1.44\cdot10^{-2}$ to $6.3\cdot10^{-5}$. The fits in Table~\ref{table:syr-fourier} single out $\chi^*_n\approx A\,n^{-1}\,0.943^{\,n}$. Only this model reaches the noise level, with RMS residual $0.0099$ and largest residual $0.029$, and its parameters are stable on subranges. The local ratio per level rises slowly, from $0.892$ ($n=16$--$23$) through $0.919$ ($n=36$--$43$) to $0.930$ ($n=72$--$79$), as the factor $n^{-1}$ predicts. The bias-free window energy $(\sum_k|\chi_n(2^k)|^2)^{1/2}$ gives the same ratios. Every fixed power, including $n^{-2.1}$, is excluded. Numerically, the decay is exponential with $c\approx0.058$ in Tao's $O(\exp(-cn))$; a proof remains open.

\paragraph{Logarithmic chart} We now attack Conjecture~\ref{conj:energy}. We do not prove it, but we rewrite $E_n$ exactly, determine the full singular set of the recursion of Proposition~\ref{prop:energy}, show that the maps $g_d$ avoid it, and locate the slowest part of the convergence of $E_n$. The maps $g_d$ become translations in the chart of the $3$-adic logarithm, which maps $1+3\Z_3$ isometrically onto $3\Z_3$ and satisfies $\log4\equiv3\pmod9$. Put $\Lambda(x)=\log(1+3x)/\log4$ for $x\in\Z_3$. Split the geometric variable as $a=c+2j$ with $c\in\{1,2\}$ and $j\ge0$. Then $c$ and $j$ are independent, $\mathbb P(c=1)=2/3$ and $\mathbb P(j=i)=\frac34\,4^{-i}$. Let $\gamma$ be the law of $-j$.

\begin{lemma}[Logarithmic chart]
\label{lem:logchart}
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] $\Lambda$ is an isometry of $\Z_3$ onto itself, $\Lambda(x)\equiv x\pmod3$, and $\Lambda\circ g_d=\Lambda+d/2$ for even $d$.
\item[(b)] The variables $L_n=\Lambda(S_n)\bmod3^n$ satisfy $L_n=\varphi_c(L'_{n-1}-j)$ with an independent copy $L'_{n-1}$ and $\varphi_c(z)=\log_4(1+3\cdot2^{-c}4^z)=\Lambda(2^{-c}4^z)$.
\item[(c)] $\varphi_c$ maps $\Z_3$ onto the class of $2^{-c}$ modulo $3$, $|\varphi_c(z)-\varphi_c(w)|=|z-w|/3$, and $\varphi_c$ is analytic but not affine. Hence the law $\nu_n$ of $L_n$ satisfies $\nu_n=\frac23\varphi_{1*}(\nu_{n-1}*\gamma)+\frac13\varphi_{2*}(\nu_{n-1}*\gamma)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) $|\log(1+3x)-\log(1+3y)|=|\log(1+3(x-y)/(1+3y))|=|3(x-y)|$ and $|\log4|=1/3$. Modulo $9$, $\log(1+3x)\equiv3x$ and $\log4\equiv3$. Since $1+3g_d(y)=4^{d/2}(1+3y)$, $\Lambda(g_d(y))=\Lambda(y)+d/2$. (b) From $S=2^{-a}(1+3S')$ and $1+3S'=4^{L'}$ we get $S=2^{-c}4^{L'-j}$. (c) By (a), $|\varphi_c(z)-\varphi_c(w)|=|4^z-4^w|=|(z-w)\log4|=|z-w|/3$, and $\varphi_c(z)\equiv2^{-c}\pmod3$. A similarity with ratio $1/3$ of $\Z_3$ into a class of Haar measure $1/3$ is onto. The derivative $\varphi_c'(z)=3\zeta/(1+3\zeta)$, $\zeta=2^{-c}4^z$, is not constant.
\end{proof}

In this chart the Syracuse law is an iterated function system of two nonlinear contractions with ratio $1/3$ and disjoint images, smeared by integer translations. In the discrete logarithm $X=\log_2S$ of the FFT of Run~L the same recursion reads $X_n=\log_2(1+3\cdot2^{X_{n-1}})-a_n$, the Syracuse step in logarithmic coordinates. Let $D_m(k)=3^m\,\mathbb P(L_m-L'_m\equiv k\pmod{3^m})$ for independent copies, $\widehat\nu_m(\eta)=\mathbb E\,e(\eta L_m/3^m)$, and
\[
 \kappa(x)=\sum_{k\ne0}4^{-|k|}e(kx)=\frac{8\cos2\pi x-2}{17-8\cos2\pi x}=\frac53\,|\widehat\gamma(x)|^2-1 .
\]

\begin{proposition}[Spectral form of the energy]
\label{prop:spectral}
$D_m(k)=\operatorname{Cor}_m(g_{2k})$, and for $n\ge0$ (with $D_0\equiv1$)
\[
 E_{n+1}=\sum_{k\ne0}4^{-|k|}D_n(k)=\sum_{\eta\bmod3^n}\kappa(\eta/3^n)\,|\widehat\nu_n(\eta)|^2=\sum_{\ell=0}^nQ_\ell,
\]
where $Q_\ell=\sum_{\omega\in(\Z/3^\ell\Z)^\times}\kappa(\omega/3^\ell)\,|\widehat\nu_\ell(\omega)|^2$.
In particular $E_{n+1}-E_n=Q_n$ and $|Q_n|\le\frac23E_n$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:logchart}(a), $L'_m\equiv L_m+k$ if and only if $S'_m\equiv g_{2k}(S_m)\pmod{3^m}$. The first equality is Proposition~\ref{prop:energy} with $d=2k$. Fourier inversion gives $D_n(k)=\sum_\eta|\widehat\nu_n(\eta)|^2e(-\eta k/3^n)$. Grouping $\eta=3^{n-\ell}\omega$ with a unit $\omega$ modulo $3^\ell$ and using $\widehat\nu_n(3^{n-\ell}\omega)=\widehat\nu_\ell(\omega)$ gives the last form. Finally $|\kappa|\le2/3$, and $\sum_\omega|\widehat\nu_\ell(\omega)|^2=E_\ell$ since $\Lambda$ preserves collision probabilities. The closed forms of $\kappa$ are geometric series, with $|\widehat\gamma(x)|^2=9/(17-8\cos2\pi x)$.
\end{proof}

The identity $\int_0^1\kappa=0$ is $\frac53\sum_i\gamma(i)^2=1$, that is $\sum_a4^{-a}=\frac13$ or $\lambda_2=1$: the $L^2$ similarity dimension of the system of Lemma~\ref{lem:logchart} is exactly $1$. The energy produced at level $n+1$ is the $\kappa$-weighted energy of level $n$, and $\kappa$ is positive for $|x|<0.21$ and negative beyond. So Conjecture~\ref{conj:energy} says that the unit-frequency energy of the logarithmic law is equidistributed in the angle $\omega/3^\ell\in\mathbb R/\Z$ up to a summable defect.

\paragraph{Renormalisation and the singular set} In Proposition~\ref{prop:energy}, $h_{a,b}=\psi_b\circ h\circ\psi_a^{-1}$ as maps of $\mathbb Q_3$, with $\psi_a(x)=(2^ax-1)/3$, and the indicator says that $h_{a,b}$ maps $\Z_3$ into itself. For a word $A=(a_1,\dots,a_k)$ let $\Psi_A=\psi_{a_k}\circ\dots\circ\psi_{a_1}$. Then $\Psi_A(x)=(2^{|A|}x-c_A)/3^k$ with $|A|=a_1+\dots+a_k$ and
\[
 c_A=\sum_{j=1}^k3^{j-1}2^{\alpha_j},\qquad\alpha_j=a_{j+1}+\dots+a_k,\qquad\alpha_1>\dots>\alpha_k=0 .
\]
Iterating the recursion $k$ times sums, over pairs $(A,B)$ of words of length $k$ and with weight $3^k2^{-|A|-|B|}$, the values $\operatorname{Cor}_{n-k}(\Psi_Bh\Psi_A^{-1})$. We call $\Psi_B^{-1}\Psi_A(y)=2^{|A|-|B|}y+(c_B-c_A)/2^{|B|}$, for words of the same length $k$, an \emph{intertwiner} of length $k$. Those of length $1$ are the maps $y\mapsto2^ey$, and $A=B$ gives the identity. A pair representing $h$ is minimal if no pair of proper prefixes represents $h$.

\begin{proposition}[Singular set and non-resonance]
\label{prop:intertwiners}
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] If $h=\Psi_B^{-1}\Psi_A$ with words of length $k$, then $\operatorname{Cor}_n(h)\ge3^k2^{-|A|-|B|}H_2(n-k)$ for $n\ge k$, and these bounds add over the minimal pairs representing $h$. Conversely, if a path of positive weight leads from $h$ to the identity, then $h$ is an intertwiner.
\item[(b)] An intertwiner with integral translation part is a map $y\mapsto2^ey$. In particular no $g_d$ with $d\ne0$ and no translation $y\mapsto y+t$ with $t\in\Z\setminus\{0\}$ is an intertwiner.
\item[(c)] Let $d\ne0$ be even and $A,B$ words of length $k$. If $|A|-|B|=d$, then $\Psi_Bg_d\Psi_A^{-1}$ is the translation by $N/3^{k+1}$, where $N=2^{|A|}+3c_A-2^{|B|}-3c_B$ satisfies $0<|N|<3^{k+1}2^{\max(|A|,|B|)-k}$. So it is the identity modulo $3^r$ only if $\max(|A|,|B|)>k+r\log_23$. If $|A|-|B|\ne d$, its linear part is $\equiv1\pmod{3^r}$ only if $\bigl||A|-|B|-d\bigr|\ge2\cdot3^{r-1}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) Along the path, the intermediate maps are the intertwiners of the suffix words, with translation parts in $\Z[1/2]\subset\Z_3$, so every indicator equals $1$. The path ends at the identity, where $\operatorname{Cor}_{n-k}(\mathrm{id})=H_2(n-k)$, and all terms are nonnegative. Minimal pairs are not prefixes of each other, so their paths are disjoint. Conversely, a path with $\Psi_Bh\Psi_A^{-1}=\mathrm{id}$ gives $h=\Psi_B^{-1}\Psi_A$.
(b) Let $\beta_j$ be the exponents of $c_B$, so $\beta_j\le|B|-j<|B|$ for $j\ge1$. An integral translation part means $c_A\equiv c_B\pmod{2^{|B|}}$. If $\alpha_i=\beta_i$ for all $i>j$, then $\sum_{i\le j}3^{i-1}2^{\alpha_i}\equiv\sum_{i\le j}3^{i-1}2^{\beta_i}\pmod{2^{|B|}}$. The right side has $2$-adic valuation $\beta_j<|B|$ and the left side $\alpha_j$, so $\alpha_j=\beta_j$. By downward induction $c_A=c_B$, and the translation part is $0$. For $g_d$ it is $(2^d-1)/3\ne0$.
(c) With $|A|=|B|+d$ and $\Psi_A^{-1}(y)=(3^ky+c_A)/2^{|A|}$, $\Psi_Bg_d\Psi_A^{-1}(y)=y+\bigl(2^{|B|}(2^d-1)/3+c_A-c_B\bigr)/3^k=y+N/3^{k+1}$. Put $X_A=2^{|A|}+3c_A=\sum_{j=0}^k3^j2^{\alpha_j}$ with $\alpha_0=|A|$. The argument of (b) without the modulus shows that $X_A$ determines $A$, so $N=X_A-X_B\ne0$. Since $\alpha_j\le|A|-j$, $0<X_A<2^{|A|}\sum_{j\le k}(3/2)^j<3^{k+1}2^{|A|-k}$. If $3^{k+1+r}\mid N$, then $3^{k+1+r}\le|N|<3^{k+1}2^{\max(|A|,|B|)-k}$. Finally, $2$ has order $2\cdot3^{r-1}$ modulo $3^r$.
\end{proof}

So the singular set of the recursion, the set of maps from which the identity is reached with positive weight, is the countable set of intertwiners. It contains maps far from the identity and from every $y\mapsto2^ey$. The translation $y\mapsto y-1/4=\Psi_{(2,1)}^{-1}\Psi_{(1,2)}$ has $\operatorname{Cor}_n\ge\frac9{64}H_2(n-2)\to\infty$. This refutes the decorrelation conjecture of the previous draft, which measured the distance to the identity and the maps $y\mapsto2^ey$ only. The mean of $\operatorname{Cor}_n(h)$ over all affine isometries $h$ of $\Z/3^n\Z$ is $1$, so by Fatou's lemma $\liminf_n\operatorname{Cor}_n(h)<\infty$ for Haar-almost every $h$. What matters are the specific maps of $E_n$. Proposition~\ref{prop:intertwiners}(b) shows that they avoid the singular set exactly, and (c) that close approaches of the walk started at $g_d$ to the identity need about $r\log_23$ letters beyond the length $k$. The missing piece is the count. Up to pairs with $||A|-|B|-d|\ge2\cdot3^{r-1}$, the weight of the pairs of length $k$ that bring $g_d$ within $3^{-r}$ of the identity is $3^k\,\mathbb P\bigl(|A|-|B|=d,\ S(B)\equiv g_d(S(A))\pmod{3^{k+r}}\bigr)$. Here $A,B$ are independent geometric words and $S(A)=\sum_{j\le k}3^{j-1}2^{-A_j}$, so that $X_A=2^{|A|}(1+3S(A))$. A bound $O(3^{-r})$, uniform in $k$, is an equidistribution statement for these truncated Syracuse sums modulo $3^{k+r}$, beyond the precision $3^k$ at which they have the law of $S_k$. Here a mixing estimate in the spirit of \cite[Propositions~1.14, 1.17]{Tao_2022} would have to enter, followed by a renewal decomposition at the close approaches. The following conjecture keeps the distance to the identity and to the maps $y\mapsto2^ey$, $\delta(h)=\sum_{e\in\Z}2^{-|e|}\min\{v_3(u-2^e),v_3(t)\}$ for $h(y)=uy+t$, and restricts the translation part as Proposition~\ref{prop:intertwiners}(b) suggests. Note that $\delta$ is infinite exactly at the identity and at the maps $y\mapsto2^ey$, and that $v_3(d/2)\le\delta(g_d)\le3v_3(d/2)$.

\begin{conjecture}[Decorrelation]
\label{conj:decor}
There are constants $A$ and $B$ with the following property: for every affine isometry $h(y)=uy+t$ with $t\in\Z$ and $\delta(h)<\infty$, $\limsup_m\operatorname{Cor}_m(h)\le A+B\,\delta(h)$.
\end{conjecture}

Conjecture~\ref{conj:decor} implies Conjecture~\ref{conj:energy}, since $\sum_d2^{-d}v_3(d/2)<\infty$, and the example $y\mapsto y-1/4$ shows that the restriction $t\in\Z$ is needed. Runs~L and~N fit it: $\operatorname{Cor}(g_d)$ is about $0.4$ to $0.9$ plus $0.47\,v_3(d/2)$; $\operatorname{Cor}(y\mapsto y+3^j)$ follows $H_2(j)$ with an almost constant offset; $\operatorname{Cor}_{15}(y\mapsto y+1)=0.450$ and $\operatorname{Cor}_{15}(y\mapsto y-1/5)=0.777$ change by less than $10^{-3}$ per level.

\paragraph{The fixed point $-1$} The slowest part of the convergence of $E_n$ sits at the fixed point $-1$ of $x\mapsto(3x+1)/2$, the $3$-adic trace of the cycle of $3x+1$ at $-1$. The branch $a=1$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:selfsim} fixes $-1$, so $f_n(-1)\ge(3/2)^n$, and the map $y\mapsto2y+1$, which fixes $-1$, is fixed by the branch $(a,b)=(1,1)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:energy}, with weight $3/4$. Two dilation rules for $f_n$ give $\operatorname{Cor}_n(g_2)=\operatorname{Cor}_n(y\mapsto y+\frac12)=\frac12\operatorname{Cor}_n(y\mapsto2y+1)$. So the term $d=2$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:energy} is bounded if and only if the difference $S'-S$ of two independent Syracuse variables has a bounded density at the point $\frac12$, and this term carries $97$ to $103\,\%$ of the increments of $E_n$ for $11\le n\le19$ (research note \texttt{syracuse\_lp}). The resonance with the factor $3/4$ appears directly in the class distribution of Run~O below.

\paragraph{Run~N} Program \texttt{log\_coordinates.py} recomputes the laws exactly for $n\le15$ in the logarithmic chart (half a minute). The identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:logchart}(c) holds to $10^{-17}$ and that of Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral} to all ten printed digits. At $\ell=15$ the shares of $E_\ell$ in the angle bins $[0,\frac1{27})$, $[\frac1{27},\frac19)$, $[\frac19,\frac15)$, $[\frac15,\frac13)$, $[\frac13,\frac12]$ are $0.0744$, $0.1488$, $0.1781$, $0.2665$, $0.3321$, against the Haar shares $0.0741$, $0.1481$, $0.1778$, $0.2667$, $0.3333$ of the units, and $Q_{15}=5.2\cdot10^{-4}$ is the residual of $\kappa$-weighted bin contributions of size up to $5.7\cdot10^{-2}$. The intertwiners grow as Proposition~\ref{prop:intertwiners}(a) predicts, and its lower bound is sharp in the growth (Table~\ref{table:syr-singular}). The maximum of $f_n$ sits at $-1$ for every $n\le15$, and $f_n(-1)(2/3)^n$ increases to $1.4620$ at $n=15$; Run~L's $\max f_{20}=4862$ equals $1.462\,(3/2)^{20}$ as well. A search over all pairs of length $k\le4$ with letters $\le8$ finds no pair representing $g_d$ ($2\le d\le20$) or $y\mapsto y+t$ ($t=1,2,-1,\frac12,-\frac15$), as Proposition~\ref{prop:intertwiners}(b) requires.

\paragraph{First-hit decomposition} By the computation in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:intertwiners}(c), the state of the walk started at $g_d$ after the words $A,B$ of length $k$ is
\[
 \Psi_Bg_d\Psi_A^{-1}(y)=2^{|B|+d-|A|}\,y+\frac{2^{|B|}\bigl(g_d(S(A))-S(B)\bigr)}{3^k} .
\]
So all indicators along the path equal $1$ if and only if $S(B)\equiv g_d(S(A))\pmod{3^k}$. The state is the identity modulo $3^r$ if and only if the \emph{hit event}
\[
 H_d(k,r)=\bigl\{|A|-|B|\equiv d\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 2\cdot3^{r-1}),\ S(B)\equiv g_d(S(A))\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 3^{k+r})\bigr\}
\]
occurs (no length condition for $r=0$). Let $w_d(k,r)=3^k\,\mathbb P(H_d(k,r))$ for independent geometric words. This is the weight of the paths of length $k$ that end within $3^{-r}$ of the identity. Then $w_d(k,0)=\operatorname{Cor}_k(g_d)$. The identity kernel $K(j,r)=w_0(j,r)$ satisfies $K(j,0)=H_2(j)$, $K(0,r)=1$ and $K\ge1$ (the diagonal $A=B$). For fixed $N$ let $F_d(k,N-k)$ be the weight of the pairs of length $k$ with $H_d(k,N-k)$ whose prefixes of length $k'<k$ all fail $H_d(k',N-k')$, the \emph{first hits} relative to $N$.

\begin{proposition}[First-hit decomposition]
\label{prop:renewal}
For $0\le k\le N$, $w_d(k,N-k)=\sum_{k'=0}^kF_d(k',N-k')\,K(k-k',N-k)$. In particular
\[
 \operatorname{Cor}_N(g_d)=\underbrace{F_d(N,0)}_{\text{regular part}}+\underbrace{\sum_{r=1}^NF_d(N-r,r)\,H_2(r)}_{\text{near-resonance part}},
\]
and $0\le F_d\le w_d$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Split a pair with $H_d(k,N-k)$ at its first hit $k'$ into a prefix and a suffix of length $k-k'$. The state after the prefix is the identity modulo $3^{N-k'}$. Since $\Psi_{B''}(x)-\Psi_{B''}(x')=2^{|B''|}(x-x')/3^{k-k'}$, the states along the suffix agree with those of the walk started at the identity modulo $3^{N-k}$ or better. So the indicators agree, and the end state is the identity modulo $3^{N-k}$ if and only if the suffix pair lies in $H_0(k-k',N-k)$. The weights multiply, and conversely every such concatenation is a first hit at $k'$. For $k=N$, $K(j,0)=H_2(j)$.
\end{proof}

The near-resonance part contains the whole growth mechanism of $H_2$, and it needs only a crude bound. Let $\varrho_d(k,r)=w_d(k,r)\,|\mathrm{Aff}(\Z/3^r\Z)|/\operatorname{Cor}_k(g_d)$, with $|\mathrm{Aff}(\Z/3^r\Z)|=2\cdot3^{2r-1}$. This is the density, relative to the uniform distribution, of the depth-$k$ states at the identity class modulo $3^r$.

For the energy itself the walk starts at the mixture of the maps $g_d$ with the weights $2^{-|d|}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:energy}. Put $w_E=\sum_{d\ne0}2^{-|d|}w_d$ and $F_E=\sum_{d\ne0}2^{-|d|}F_d$. Then $w_E(k,0)=E_{k+1}$, and by Proposition~\ref{prop:renewal}
\[
 E_{N+1}=F_E(N,0)+\sum_{r=1}^NF_E(N-r,r)\,H_2(r).
\]
The mixture puts only the weight $w_E(0,r)=2/(4^{3^r}-1)$ on the maps that are the identity modulo $3^r$, so no depth has to be excluded. Let $\varrho_E(k,r)=w_E(k,r)\,|\mathrm{Aff}(\Z/3^r\Z)|/E_{k+1}$ and $\varrho^*_E(r)=\sup_{k\ge0}\varrho_E(k,r)$.

\begin{corollary}[Conditional bound for the energy]
\label{cor:energy-bootstrap}
Let $c_E=\frac32\sum_r\varrho^*_E(r)(5/27)^r$. If $c_E<1$ and $F_E(N,0)\le F^*_E$ for all $N\ge1$, then $\sup_nE_n\le\max\bigl(\frac23,F^*_E/(1-c_E)\bigr)$. In particular Conjecture~\ref{conj:energy} holds, and the limiting density lies in $L^p(\Z_3)$ for every $p<2$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:lp}).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:renewal}, $F_E(N-r,r)\le w_E(N-r,r)=\frac32\varrho_E(N-r,r)9^{-r}E_{N-r+1}$. Moreover $H_2(r)\le(5/3)^r$, since $E_n\le\frac23H_2(n-1)$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral} and $D_{n-1}(k)\le H_2(n-1)$ (Cauchy--Schwarz). Hence $E_{N+1}\le F^*_E+c_E\max_{1\le m\le N}E_m$ for $N\ge1$, and $E_1=\frac23$; induct on $N$.
\end{proof}

So Conjecture~\ref{conj:energy} follows from an anti-concentration of the energy walk at the identity ($c_E<1$) and a bound for its regular part, the weight of the paths that never come close to the identity. A large-deviation bound settles the far range $r\ge2.71k$ of the first condition for each $d$ (research note \texttt{syracuse\_lp}). The one-step weights of the recursion depend only on the class $c=(u\bmod3,t\bmod3)$ of the state $h(y)=uy+t$: $W(1,0)=\frac53$, $W(2,0)=W(2,1)=\frac43$, $W(1,1)=W(1,2)=\frac23$, $W(2,2)=\frac13$, with mean $1$. Let $\nu_k$ be the distribution of the depth-$k$ states over these six classes, weighted with the path weights. Then exactly
\[
 \frac{\operatorname{Cor}_{k+1}(g_d)}{\operatorname{Cor}_k(g_d)}-1=\sum_cW(c)\Bigl(\nu_k(c)-\frac16\Bigr).
\]
So the regular part is bounded if and only if the class distribution becomes uniform summably fast. This is a mixing statement in the spirit of \cite[Propositions~1.14, 1.17]{Tao_2022}, and it is the open core of Conjecture~\ref{conj:energy}.

\paragraph{Run~O} Program \texttt{congruence\_counts.py} computes $w_d(k,r)$ exactly up to floating point for $k+r\le N$. For each of the length phases $\theta=j/162$ it computes the complex law of $S(A)\bmod3^N$ weighted with $e(\theta(|A|-k))$, by the recursion $S(A)=2^{-a}(1+3S(A'))$ started from the point mass at $0$ modulo $3^{N-k}$. An inverse Fourier transform in $\theta$ then gives the law of $|A|-|B|$ on the coincidence event. The program agrees with a brute-force walk for $N\le3$ and with Run~L to $5\cdot10^{-11}$. It ran locally for $N\le13$ and on the cluster for $N\le17$ (42 minutes, 82 processes, 216~GB). Table~\ref{table:syr-runO} gives the split of Proposition~\ref{prop:renewal}.
\begin{itemize}
\item For $d=2$ the regular part decreases and the near-resonance part increases. Their increments have opposite signs and shrink by the local factors $0.72$ down to $0.655$, and about $0.77$. So the local factors $0.87$--$0.92$ of Run~L are a near-cancellation of two faster parts. For $d=4$ both parts change by less than $0.002$ from $N=9$ on.
\item Within the near-resonance part, the terms of precision $r$ converge more slowly as $r$ grows. Their increments shrink by about $0.70$ ($r=1$), $0.83$ ($r=2$), $0.9$ ($r=3$) and $1.0$ ($r=4$), and the term $r=5$ still grows, while their amplitudes fall like $9^{-r}$. The congruence $|A|-|B|\equiv d\pmod{2\cdot3^{r-1}}$ relaxes diffusively, on a time scale of order $9^r$. A two-component extrapolation predicts the local factors $0.863$, $0.846$, $0.832$ for $n=18$, $19$, $20$, against $0.874$, $0.868$, $0.866$ in Run~L. So the convergence of $E_n$ is probably slower than geometric in the end, which does not affect its boundedness.
\item The factor $\varrho_d=\varrho_t\varrho_{\mathrm{len}}$ splits into the $t$-part, $3^{k+r}\mathbb P(S(B)\equiv g_d(S(A))\bmod3^{k+r})/\operatorname{Cor}_k(g_d)$, and the length part. The $t$-part stays at or below equidistribution once $k\ge7$ ($\le1.004$). The length part grows with $r$, up to $7.2$ for $d=4$ and $r=7$. Hence $\max\varrho_4=4.96$, and the anti-concentration constants $c_d=\frac32\sum_r\sup_k\varrho_d(k,r)(5/27)^r$ of a single $g_d$, the analogues of $c_E$, need the weights $(5/27)^r$. On the computed range $c_2=0.33$ and $c_4=0.38$.
\item The class distribution for $d=2$ starts concentrated in the class $(2,1)$ of $y\mapsto2y+1$ ($\nu_1=0.60$). Its excess decays by the factors $0.749$--$0.752$ for $8\le k\le13$. This is the resonance at $-1$, seen directly. A mean-field (Ulam) discretisation of the walk, which averages over the next digit, sees only the diffusion of the exponent of the linear part (eigenvalues $1/(5-4\cos(2\pi j/L))$, $L=2\cdot3^{s-1}$) and misses the resonance. A certified contraction must therefore act on the correlations themselves.
\end{itemize}


\paragraph{Run~O, all $d$} In the logarithmic chart every $g_d$ is a translation. So program \texttt{energy\_renewal.py} obtains the coincidence weights of all $d$ at once, from one cyclic autocorrelation per length phase. For $N\le13$ and $|d|\le80$ it reproduces the hit weights of Run~O to $10^{-16}$ and the identity $w_E(k,0)=H_2(k+1)-H_2(k)$ to $10^{-14}$. The energy walk is anti-concentrated at the identity: $\varrho_E\le0.97$ throughout, and $c_E=0.32$. Its regular part $F_E(N,0)$ decreases monotonically from $0.445$ at $N=2$ to $0.341$ at $N=13$, while the near-resonance part grows to $0.129$. On the computed range Corollary~\ref{cor:energy-bootstrap} gives $E_n\le\max(\frac23,0.651)=\frac23$, against $E_{14}=0.470$. For single $d$, $c_d$ lies between $0.31$ and $0.68$ for $37$ of the $40$ values $2\le d\le80$, and $\operatorname{Cor}_N(g_d)$ is bounded by a constant multiple of $H_2(v_3(d/2))$ uniformly in $d$ for $N\le15$, as Conjecture~\ref{conj:decor} predicts (research note \texttt{syracuse\_lp}).

\paragraph{What a proof needs} Two observations narrow down the missing mixing statement. First, no certificate at a fixed finite precision can bound the correlations, because the intertwiners are dense: every class of $\mathrm{Aff}(\Z/3^m\Z)$ contains one. Indeed, let $B$ agree with $A$ except for $b_1=a_1+\varepsilon$ and $b_k=a_k+\delta$. Then $\Psi_B^{-1}\Psi_A(y)=2^{-\varepsilon-\delta}y+(2^\delta-1)\,2^{a_k-|B|}c'$ with $c'=\sum_{j<k}3^{j-1}2^{\gamma_j}$ and $\gamma_j=a_{j+1}+\dots+a_{k-1}$. For $k\ge m+2$, choosing $\gamma_1>\dots>\gamma_m$ one at a time, each modulo $6$, makes $c'$ any unit modulo $3^m$. The letter $a_1$ fixes $|B|$ modulo $2\cdot3^{m-1}$ without changing $c'$, $\delta$ gives the translation part its $3$-adic valuation ($\delta=0$ for the translation $0$, $\delta$ odd for a unit, $\delta=2\cdot3^{v-1}$ for valuation $v$), and $\varepsilon$ fixes the linear part. For $m\le3$, intertwiners of length $\max(2,m)$ already suffice (program \texttt{intertwiner\_density.py} checks both statements). Since $\operatorname{Cor}_n$ is unbounded at every intertwiner by Proposition~\ref{prop:intertwiners}(a), a bound $\operatorname{Cor}_n(h)\le V(h)$ with $V$ depending only on $h$ modulo $3^m$ forces $V=\infty$. For the regular part alone, a certificate has to hold for every value of the next, unknown digit, while already the mean-field operator of Run~O, which averages over that digit, has a spectral gap of order $9^{-m}$ at precision $3^m$. The contraction therefore has to come from the distribution of the digits, that is, from a mixing theorem. Second, summable rates suffice: by the identity for $\operatorname{Cor}_{k+1}(g_d)/\operatorname{Cor}_k(g_d)$ above, $\sum_k\max_c|\nu_k(c)-\frac16|<\infty$ bounds $\operatorname{Cor}_N(g_d)$, and Tao's fine-scale mixing \cite[Proposition~1.14]{Tao_2022} has the rate $m^{-A}$ for every $A$. But it is an $L^1$ statement for a single variable, whereas $\nu_k$ is the law of a pair conditioned on its coincidence modulo $3^k$. A direct transfer pays the factor $\max f_k\ge f_k(-1)\ge(3/2)^k$. What is missing is a pair version of Tao's fine-scale mixing in which the spikes at $-1$ and their images under the self-similarity are removed. The exponential decay of the largest Fourier coefficients (Run~M) would need more: Tao expects such a rate but does not attempt it \cite[Remark~1.15]{Tao_2022}.
